### 1. Root-Cause Remediation Applied During Verification
1. **Exited Docker Containers on `.164` Post-Reboot**:
- Started exited containers on `.164`: `forgejo` (`:3000`), `litellm-proxy` (`:4000`), `devpi-server` (`:3141`), `tei-embeddings` (`:8081`), `prometheus` (`:9090`), `grafana-dashboards` (`:3001`), `traefik-ingress` (`:80/:8080`), `kafka` (`:9092`), `code-server` (`:8443`), `alertmanager` (`:9093`), `searxng-search` (`:8888`), `devdocs-mirror` (`:8088`), `cadvisor-metrics` (`:8085`), `node-exporter` (`:9100`), `docker-mirror` (`:5000`), `verdaccio` (`:4873`), `apt-cacher-ng` (`:3142`).
2. **Stale Pre-Reboot `iptables -t nat DOCKER` DNAT Rules on `.164`**:
- Found 19 stale pre-reboot `DNAT` rules at the top of `iptables -t nat DOCKER` on `.164` shadowing the newly started container bridge IPs (`172.17.x.x` – `172.21.x.x`), which caused `http://10.0.70.164:<port>` (for `:3000`, `:3001`, `:3141`, `:4000`, `:8081`, `:9090`, etc.) to refuse connections while `127.0.0.1:<port>` worked.
- Purged all 19 stale duplicate `DNAT` rules from `iptables -t nat DOCKER`. Immediately restored `HTTP 200` on `10.0.70.164:<port>` both locally on `.164` and remotely from `.82`.
3. **Port `:8000 -> :8082` NAT Redirect on `.72`**:
- On `.72`, `llama-tier1-alpha.service` (`Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf`) listens on `:8082`. Restored `iptables -t nat` `PREROUTING` and `OUTPUT` `REDIRECT` rules mapping `:8000 -> :8082` so both `http://10.0.70.72:8000` and `http://10.0.70.72:8082` respond `HTTP 200`.

### 1. `dspark-DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-Q8_0.gguf` -> `Q4_K_M` Quantization (`164` Prep)
- **Host**: `10.0.70.83`
- **Source Path**: `/mnt/ai-models/deepseek-v4-flash/dspark-DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-Q8_0.gguf` (`10,386.28 MiB`, `4.39 BPW`, 81 tensors: `41x f32`, `25x q8_0`, `6x bf16`, `9x mxfp4`)
- **Output Path**: `/mnt/ai-models/deepseek-v4-flash/dspark-DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-Q4_K_M.gguf` (`11,729,015,904 bytes` / `11,180.65 MiB`, `4.73 BPW`)
- **Command Executed**:
```bash
LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/opt/llama-rpc /opt/llama-rpc/llama-quantize --allow-requantize \
/mnt/ai-models/deepseek-v4-flash/dspark-DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-Q8_0.gguf \
/mnt/ai-models/deepseek-v4-flash/dspark-DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-Q4_K_M.gguf \
Q4_K_M 24
```
- **Quantization Log Summary** (`/var/tmp/quantize-dspark-q4km.log`):
```text
llama_model_quantize_impl: model size  = 10386.28 MiB (4.39 BPW)
llama_model_quantize_impl: quant size  = 11180.65 MiB (4.73 BPW)
llama_quantize: quantize time = 2216731.78 ms
llama_quantize:    total time = 2216731.78 ms
```
- **Critical Architecture / VRAM Note for Agent-164**:
- `dspark-DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-Q8_0.gguf` (`general.architecture = dflash`, `dflash.block_count = 3`, `general.size_label = 256x594M`) is a hybrid model whose 9 routed MoE expert tensors (`blk.{0,1,2}.ffn_{gate,up,down}_exps.weight`, each `[4096, 2048, 256]`) are **already quantized as `mxfp4` (`4.25 BPW`, `1088.00 MiB` each = `9,792 MiB`)** in both the `BF16` and `Q8_0` source GGUFs, while only the 25 dense/shared-expert/attention tensors (`fc.weight`, `attn_q_a`, `attn_q_b`, `attn_kv`, `attn_output_a`, `attn_output_b`, `ffn_*_shexp`) were `q8_0` (`~430 MiB`) and `markov_w1`/`markov_w2` were `bf16` (`126.25 MiB`).
- Converting to `Q4_K_M` reduces the 25 dense/attention/markov tensors from `~556 MiB` (`q8_0`/`bf16`) down to **`~245 MiB` (`q4_K`/`q6_K`)**, while converting the 9 `mxfp4` (`4.25 BPW`) expert tensors to `8x q4_K` (`1152.00 MiB` each, `4.50 BPW`) + `1x q6_K` (`1680.00 MiB`, `6.56 BPW`).
- **Recommended T4 (`15.3 GiB`) Speculative Launch Flags**: Pass **`--cpu-moe-draft` (`-cmoed`)** alongside `-md /mnt/ai-models/deepseek-v4-flash/dspark-DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-Q4_K_M.gguf` so the 9 draft MoE expert tensors reside in host RAM while **only `~245 MiB` of `Q4_K_M` draft attention/fc/markov weights reside in T4 VRAM** alongside `DeepSeek-V4-Flash Q2` attention + `-c 16384` `q8_0` KV cache.

### 1. Root Cause Analysis of Missing Agents on the Terminal TV Wall (`:4083`)
1. **Hardcoded DOM Slice Cap (`48` panes max)**:
- In `bd-web-terminal-drawer.py`, `renderTvWall()` previously had `const limit = GRID_MODE === '2x2' ? 4 : (GRID_MODE === '3x3' ? 9 : 48);`, which truncated any agent beyond index 48 even when `All Panes` mode was selected.
2. **Backend Discovery Caps & Missing Sources**:
- `_discover_source_a_agy_subagents()` previously capped at `max_agents=10` and did not deduplicate or surface all active subagents (`Agent-151`..`Agent-156`, `AGY-PM-Dispatcher`, `Agent-37`, `Agent-105`..`Agent-150`).
- `_discover_source_c_gpu_benchmarks()` previously returned only 1 static benchmark summary tile instead of the 6 live remote GPU & compute engines (`.83:18080`, `.72:8000`, `.137:11434`, `.125:11434`, `.228:11434`, `.82` 64-core Ramdisk Pytest).
- **OpenWebUI Autonomous Agents (`:8090` / `:8080`)** (`16` agents: `agent-council-relief-commander` + the 15 specialist agents) were not discovered by `/api/tv-wall`.
- **230-Swarm Mega-Blitz Cohorts & Driver State** (`/var/tmp/bd-autonomous-swarm-driver-state.json`, `/var/tmp/fleet_telemetry_live.json`, and `master_230_swarm_mega_blitz_ledger.json` Cohorts I–VIII) were not surfaced as TV Wall tiles.
- Local `tmux` sessions `bd-ux-claude-B` and `bd-kimi-swarm-1..4` were not spawned on `.164`.

### 1. Architecture & Endpoints Implemented on `:4079` (`bd-openwebui-tool-gateway.py` + `bd_openwebui_27vm_terminal.py`)
### A. CORS & Browser Direct-Connection Compatibility
- Every HTTP response (`200`, `204`, `4xx`, `5xx`) and `OPTIONS *` preflight on `:4079` emits:
- `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`
- `Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, POST, PUT, DELETE, PATCH, OPTIONS, HEAD`
- `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: *`
- `Access-Control-Expose-Headers: *`
- Supports both OpenWebUI's backend proxy (`/api/v1/terminals/<id>/*`) and browser-direct connections (`User Settings -> Integrations -> Open Terminal` & `Tools`).
### B. All-27-VM Open Terminal Multiplexer (`/vm/all/*`) & Individual VM Terminals (`/vm/<vm_target>/*`)
Each of the **28 Open Terminal endpoints** (`/vm/all` + 27 `/vm/<ip>`) implements the full OpenWebUI Open Terminal specification (`DXIE4il6.js` + `open_webui/routers/terminals.py` + `open_webui/utils/tools.py`):
- `GET .../openapi.json` & `GET .../terminal/openapi.json` (OpenAPI 3.1.0 spec with `x-open-terminal` extension and `operationId`s: `run_command`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `list_files`, `search_files`, `glob_files`, `grep_matches`, `get_system_info`, `exec_on_any_vm`)
- `GET .../api/config` (`{"status": true, "version": "1.2.0", "type": "terminal", "features": {"files": true, "terminal": true, "skills": true, "execute": true, "notebooks": true, "system": true}}`)
- `GET .../system` (live hostname, IP, role, OS, CPU/RAM/load telemetry)
- `GET/POST .../files/cwd` (per-session working directory tracking; defaults to `/vms` for `/vm/all` and each VM's default directory for `/vm/<ip>`)

### 1. Swarm #19 & #C14: vSphere Snapshot TimeCapsule (`/home/mboyle/bin/bd-vm-timecapsule`)
### Design & Safety Invariants
- **vCenter Binding**: Loads `/home/mboyle/.config/bd/vsphere.env` and `/home/mboyle/bin/lib_vsphere_govc.py`, connecting to `vc04` (`10.0.20.70`) via `pyVmomi` (`SmartConnect` over TLS port 443) with automatic fallback to `govc`.
- **Subcommands**:
- `bd-vm-timecapsule --list [--json]`: Walks `vm.snapshot.rootSnapshotList` and `vm.layoutEx.file` across all 54 VMs on `vc04`, reporting snapshot names, UTC creation timestamps, delta disk sizes in MB, TTL hours, and expiration status.
- `bd-vm-timecapsule --snapshot <vm-name> [--ttl-hours 72] [--reason "..."] [--dry-run]`: Creates a thin, memory-free (`memory=False`, `quiesce=True` with automatic `quiesce=False` fallback) snapshot named `boylenet-timecapsule-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>-ttl<N>h` with structured JSON metadata in the snapshot description.
- `bd-vm-timecapsule --prune-expired [--dry-run] [--json]`: Evaluates only snapshots matching `^boylenet-timecapsule-(\d{8}T\d{6}Z)-ttl(\d+)h$`. Any snapshot older than its embedded TTL is pruned via `RemoveSnapshot_Task(removeChildren=False, consolidate=True)` so vSAN never accumulates stale delta disks while preserving all non-TimeCapsule snapshots untouched.
### Live Verification Output (`bd-vm-timecapsule --list`)

- **`SQLiteWalStateStore` (`#C5`)**:
- Configures `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;`, `PRAGMA synchronous=NORMAL;`, `PRAGMA busy_timeout=30000;`, `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;`, `PRAGMA wal_autocheckpoint=1000;`, and explicit `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` / `COMMIT` / `ROLLBACK` transactions with bounded exponential backoff.
- **9 Verification Tests**:
1. `test_wal_pragma_and_schema_initialization` — Verifies `journal_mode=wal`, `busy_timeout >= 30000`, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `foreign_keys=1`, and clean table initialization (`swarm_tasks`, `wal_audit_log`, `swarm_counters`).
2. `test_16_concurrent_writer_threads_zero_locked_errors` — Exercises **16 concurrent writer threads** (`640` total write transactions across unique and contested hot slots) + **4 concurrent reader threads** querying atomic WAL MVCC snapshots simultaneously with **zero `OperationalError: database is locked`**.
3. `test_crash_safe_atomic_transaction_rollback` — Injects a mid-transaction runtime failure after partial upsert and proves `ROLLBACK` preserves exact pre-transaction state with zero dirty rows leaked.
4. `test_wal_checkpoint_and_snapshot_recovery` — Executes `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` (`busy == 0`, `log_frames == checkpointed_frames`) and verifies durable state reload across fresh connections.
5. `test_token_bucket_burst_and_refill_precision` — Verifies initial burst capacity, non-blocking `try_acquire`, monotonic refill precision, and bounded blocking `acquire`.
6. `test_adaptive_aimd_429_backoff_and_200_recovery` — Verifies multiplicative decrease (`×0.5` down to `min_rate`) and `Retry-After` cooldown window on HTTP `429`/`503`, additive recovery (`+step` up to `max_rate`) on HTTP `200` streaks, and `sync_from_headers()` alignment.
7. `test_16_thread_concurrent_rate_limiter_fairness_and_thread_safety` — Exercises **16 concurrent threads** (`240` token acquisitions under live throttle/recovery perturbations) with zero race conditions and invariant `0.0 <= tokens <= capacity`.

- Tier 2: Pure-Python multi-harmonic formant speech synthesizer fallback (`synthesize_harmonic_wav`) returning in **`<8ms`** so `POST /v1/audio/speech` never fails even completely offline.
2. **Speech-to-Text (`POST /v1/audio/transcriptions` & `POST /v1/audio/translations`)**:
- OpenAI-compatible Whisper endpoint accepting `multipart/form-data` audio uploads (`wav`, `mp3`, `webm`, `ogg`), raw audio streams, or JSON base64 payloads + browser WebSpeech hybrid transcripts.

3. **Vision & Screenshot-to-Code (`POST /v1/vision/analyze`)**:
- Accepts base64 screenshots (`Ctrl+V` clipboard paste, drag-and-drop, or API payload) + operator prompt + mode (`screenshot-to-code`, `ui-bug-diagnosis`, `ocr-dom-extract`, `architecture-review`).

### 1. Architecture & 3-Stage Autonomous Pipeline
1. **Stage 1 — Static Fast-Fail Gates (`<25 ms`)**:

- **Secret & Credential Scanner**: Regex scan across added diff lines (`+...`) for AWS Access Key IDs, Private Key headers, GitHub/OAuth tokens, OpenAI/Anthropic production keys, Slack tokens, and hardcoded password assignments.
2. **Stage 2 — Parallel Local GPU Multi-Lens Review (`$0.00` Marginal Token Cost via `http://10.0.70.228:4000/v1`)**:
- **Lens 1 — Behavioral & Logic Defect Lens (`qwen3-coder-30b-a3b`)**: Evaluates diffs under **Fleet Rule 86 (Protocol F)** (`BOARD` vs `REFUTE` vs `BOARD-WITH-ADVISORY`), checking only for demonstrable behavioral defects, runtime crashes, regressions, and race conditions.
- **Lens 2 — OffSec & Security Red-Team Lens (`cyberstrike-offsec-35b` with fallback `qwen3-coder-30b-a3b`)**: Audits diffs for command injection (`CWE-78`), arbitrary code execution (`CWE-95`), hardcoded credentials (`CWE-798`), SSRF, path traversal, and auth bypass.
3. **Stage 3 — Gitea Feedback Loop & Cyber-Glass Web UI (`:4092`)**:
- Automatically posts commit statuses (`boylenet/prereview-2lens-swarm`) and structured Markdown review comments back to Gitea (`http://10.0.70.164:3000`).

## 126. [AGENT54-25GBE-TENSOR-TRANSPORT-TUNING-RECEIPTS.md] Agent-54 (Swarm #6): 25GbE VLAN 75 Low-Latency Tensor Transport Tuning Receipts
- **Source Path**: `/home/mboyle/bd-persist/harness-work/O1789/infra-upgrade/AGENT54-25GBE-TENSOR-TRANSPORT-TUNING-RECEIPTS.md` | **Updated**: `2026-10-18T15:41:32Z` | **SHA-256**: `2d242e8450fc4fac`

- **Date (UTC)**: `2026-10-18T15:40:09Z`
- **Seat**: `Agent-54` (Swarm #6 — 25GbE RoCE v2 & Low-Latency Tensor Transport Tuning across `10.0.75.11..14`)
- **Target Interconnect**: Dedicated 25GbE Tensor VLAN 75 (`10.0.75.11..14/24`, `vmxnet3`, MTU `9000`)
---
### 1. Ground-Truth Node & NIC Mapping (`10.0.75.11..14`)
> [!IMPORTANT]
> Live NIC inspection (`ip -br -4 addr` & `ss -tanp | grep 50052`) verified the exact Management IP (`10.0.70.x`) to Tensor VLAN 75 IP (`10.0.75.x`) mapping across the 4 GPU nodes (`10.0.70.228` holds `.13` and `10.0.70.125` holds `.14`):
| Hostname | Mgmt IP (`VLAN 70`) | Tensor IP (`VLAN 75`) | Interface | Driver (`bus-info`) | MTU | Queues (`TX/RX`) | `llama-rpc-worker.service` (`:50052`) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `aidev` | `10.0.70.83` | `10.0.75.11/24` | `ens33` (`enp2s1`) | `vmxnet3` (`1.7.0.0-k-NAPI`, `0000:02:01.0`) | `9000` (max `9190`) | `32 / 32` | `active` (`LISTEN 10.0.75.11:50052`, `pid=198759`) |
| `ai-srv01` | `10.0.70.72` | `10.0.75.12/24` | `ens33` (`enp2s1`) | `vmxnet3` (`1.7.0.0-k-NAPI`, `0000:02:01.0`) | `9000` (max `9190`) | `32 / 32` | `active` (`LISTEN 10.0.75.12:50052`, `pid=1669`) |
| `ai-ollama01` | `10.0.70.228` | `10.0.75.13/24` | `ens33` (`enp2s1`) | `vmxnet3` (`1.7.0.0-k-NAPI`, `0000:02:01.0`) | `9000` (max `9190`) | `32 / 32` | `active` (`LISTEN 10.0.75.13:50052`, `pid=72333`) |
| `ai-infer01` | `10.0.70.125` | `10.0.75.14/24` | `ens33` (`enp2s1`) | `vmxnet3` (`1.7.0.0-k-NAPI`, `0000:02:01.0`) | `9000` (max `9190`) | `32 / 32` | `active` (`LISTEN 10.0.75.14:50052`, `pid=22951`) |
---
### 2. Diagnostic Findings & Bottlenecks Remediated
1. **`vmxnet3` Jumbo Ring Starvation (`RX Jumbo: 512` vs `4096` max)**:
- Although `ens33` had `mtu 9000` and standard `RX: 4096 / TX: 4096` rings, `vmxnet3` routes MTU 9000 frames through the separate **`RX Jumbo`** ring (`rx-jumbo`), which was still at its default **`512`** (`RX Mini` at **`128`** vs `2048` max). Under multi-stream or bursty tensor transfers, the 512-slot jumbo ring overflowed, causing `1,754–9,550` TCP retransmissions per 2-second burst.
- **Fix**: Expanded `rx 4096 tx 4096 rx-jumbo 4096 rx-mini 2048` on all 4 nodes.
2. **Interrupt Coalescing Latency (`rx-usecs: 250`)**:
- `ethtool -c ens33` showed `rx-usecs: 250` across all 4 nodes, introducing up to 0.25 ms of artificial RX interrupt delay on small RPC control/sync packets, inflating 64B ping tail latency to `0.962–1.070 ms` and jitter (`mdev`) to `0.189–0.214 ms`.
... *(truncated in digest; full receipt at `/home/mboyle/bd-persist/harness-work/O1789/infra-upgrade/AGENT54-25GBE-TENSOR-TRANSPORT-TUNING-RECEIPTS.md`)*

### 1. Architecture & O1740 Dynamic Capability Routing Implementation
1. **Telemetry & Live Health Ingestion**:
- Continuously parses `/home/mboyle/bd-persist/POOL_STATE.tsv` (`state`, `pct` / `five_hour_pct`, `weekly_pct`, `resets_at`, `five_hour_remaining_pct`, `quota_observed_at`).
- Performs live health probes across:
- `Claude ABCD` (`/home/mboyle/.claude-{a,b,c,d}/.credentials.json` freshness).
- `codex` (CLI binary, `~/.codex/config.toml`, `~/.codex/hooks.json`).
- `kimi` (CLI binary / gateway `kimi-for-coding`, monthly quota extraction from evidence).
- `AGY` (`~/.gemini/config/hooks.json` shield status + subagent quota).
- `local-4xt4` (`http://10.0.70.164:4010/health` GPU Orchestrator + `http://10.0.70.228:4000/health/liveliness` LiteLLM Gateway).
- Tracks active heavy-lifter seats via `tmux list-sessions` against the O1740 30-seat global cap.
2. **Role-Tier Routing & Autonomous Failover (`--recommend <role>`)**:
- **Command Squad** (`integrator`, `dispatch`, `audit`, `adjudicator`, `trainer`, `lensrouter`, `pm`):
- Picks the `OK` Opus-tier pool (`A`, `B`, `C`, `D`) with the lowest `five_hour_pct` (`<85%`), tie-breaking by lowest `weekly_pct`, active seat count, and role affinity.
- **Autonomous Failover**: When all Claude pools are `>=85%` or `OFF-PACE`/`LIMITED`/`UNKNOWN`, fails over cleanly to `codex` (`gpt-6-astra`) and then `hermes` (`mimo-v2.6-pro-1024b` / `glm-5.3-753b`).
- **Heavy Lifters** (`worker`, `builder`, `fixer`, `review-*`):
- Picks the most capable admitting pool (`five_hour_pct < 80%`), balancing load across `Claude ABCD` (`claude-opus-5-5`), `Codex` (`gpt-6-sol` / `gpt-6-astra`), `Kimi` (`kimi-for-coding`), and `Local 4xT4` (`qwen3-coder-30b-a3b` / `swift-qwen3.8-flash-next`).
- **Lightweight Investigators** (`investigator`, `probe`, `cavecrew-*`):
- Picks the fast-model pool with the highest idle quota (`max(100 - five_hour_pct)`) or Local Tier 1 (`local-4xt4` running `qwen3-coder-30b-a3b` / `swift-qwen3.8-flash-next` at `$0.00` marginal cost).

## 129. [AGENT46-LITELLM-POSTGRES-DB-RECEIPTS.md] Agent-46 Receipt: LiteLLM PostgreSQL Database & Admin UI Provisioning (`.164:4000` & `.228:4000`)
- **Source Path**: `/home/mboyle/bd-persist/harness-work/O1789/infra-upgrade/AGENT46-LITELLM-POSTGRES-DB-RECEIPTS.md` | **Updated**: `2026-10-18T14:31:01Z` | **SHA-256**: `c7e88c4781fc446f`

**WRITTEN-AT**: `2026-10-18T14:30:30Z`
**STATUS**: `PASS (0 "Not connected to DB!" errors; 77 Prisma tables synced; HTTP 200 on /v2/login, /v2/user/info, /v2/model/info, /key/list, /v1/models)`
---
### 1. Root Cause of `"Authentication Error, Not connected to DB!"`
1. **Missing `DATABASE_URL`**: Both `litellm-proxy` on `10.0.70.164:4000` and `litellm-proxy` on `10.0.70.228:4000` were running `ghcr.io/berriai/litellm:main-latest` without `DATABASE_URL` in `/home/mboyle/infra/support-layer/litellm/.env` or `general_settings.database_url` in `config.yaml`. When `/v2/login` ran `user_update()` and `generate_key_helper_fn()`, `prisma_client` was `None`, raising `Exception("Not connected to DB!")`.
2. **Incomplete `custom_auth.py` Principal**: `/home/mboyle/infra/support-layer/litellm/custom_auth.py` previously returned `UserAPIKeyAuth(api_key=api_key or "sk-boylenet-local")` with `user_id=None` and `user_role=None`, which caused Admin UI endpoints (`/v2/user/info`, `/user/list`, `/config/list`) to reject requests even with a database connected.
3. **Docker Bridge PostgreSQL HBA Rule**: `/etc/postgresql/16/main/pg_hba.conf` on `10.0.70.164` lacked an entry for `172.16.0.0/12`, blocking Docker bridge containers (`172.17.0.x`) on `.164` from authenticating to `10.0.70.164:5432`.
---
### 2. Remedies & Provisioning
1. **PostgreSQL 16 (`10.0.70.164:5432`)**:
- Added `host all all 172.16.0.0/12 scram-sha-256` to `/etc/postgresql/16/main/pg_hba.conf` and reloaded PostgreSQL (`SELECT pg_reload_conf();`).
- Created role `litellm` with password `boylenet-admin` (`SUPERUSER CREATEDB LOGIN`).
- Created databases `litellm` (shared primary DB for `.164` and `.228`) and `litellm_228` owned by `litellm`.
- Synced all **77 Prisma tables** (`LiteLLM_UserTable`, `LiteLLM_VerificationToken`, `LiteLLM_TeamTable`, `LiteLLM_SpendLogs`, `LiteLLM_ProxyModelTable`, etc.) via `PrismaManager.setup_database()`.
- Seeded `LiteLLM_UserTable` (`user_id='default_user_id'`, `user_email='admin'`, `user_role='proxy_admin'`), `LiteLLM_TeamTable` (`litellm-dashboard`, `boylenet-fleet`), and `LiteLLM_VerificationToken` with hashed tokens for `sk-fleet-local`, `sk-boylenet-local`, and `sk-local`.
2. **LiteLLM Configuration (`/home/mboyle/infra/support-layer/litellm/` on `.164` and `.228`)**:
- `config.yaml`: Added `database_url: os.environ/DATABASE_URL` and `store_model_in_db: true` under `general_settings`.

### 1. Misconfigurations Discovered & Remediated
1. **Controller LiteLLM (`10.0.70.164:4000`) Missing `custom_auth.py` & Stale In-Memory Catalog (`18` vs `38` Models)**:
- **Defect**: `/home/mboyle/infra/support-layer/litellm/config.yaml` on `10.0.70.164` was missing `tier1-cyberstrike-35b-q4km` (37 models on disk vs 38 on `10.0.70.228`), the running container had not been reloaded since prior edits (serving only 18 models in `/v1/models`), and `general_settings.custom_auth: custom_auth.user_api_key_auth` caused `ImportError: Could not import user_api_key_auth from custom_auth` on container restart because `/app/custom_auth.py` was not present or mounted on `10.0.70.164`.
- **Fix**:
- Added `tier1-cyberstrike-35b-q4km` (`ollama/cyberstrike:35b-a3b-q4_K_M` -> `http://10.0.10.137:11434`) to `/home/mboyle/infra/support-layer/litellm/config.yaml` on `10.0.70.164`.
- Installed `/home/mboyle/infra/support-layer/litellm/custom_auth.py` on `10.0.70.164` matching `10.0.70.228`.
- Updated `/home/mboyle/infra/support-layer/litellm/run.sh` on `10.0.70.164` to bind-mount `-v "$D/custom_auth.py:/app/custom_auth.py:ro"` and recreated `litellm-proxy`.
- Verified `http://10.0.70.164:4000/v1/models` now exposes all **38** models (`HTTP 200`), matching `http://10.0.70.228:4000/v1/models` (**38** models), and `http://10.0.70.164:8090/api/models` now exposes **60** total models (`38` LiteLLM + `17` Ollama + `5` custom/arena).
2. **AI Vault A OpenWebUI (`10.0.70.72:8080`) Unconfigured OpenAI/LiteLLM Upstream**:
- **Defect**: `http://10.0.70.72:8080/openai/config` had `OPENAI_API_BASE_URLS: ["https://api.openai.com/v1"]` with an empty API key `""`, exposing only 7 local Ollama/Arena models (`qwen2.5-coder:7b`, `bge-m3:latest`, etc.) and failing to surface the fleet LiteLLM model catalog.
- **Fix**: Updated `POST http://10.0.70.72:8080/openai/config/update` with `OPENAI_API_BASE_URLS: ["http://10.0.70.164:4000/v1"]` and `OPENAI_API_KEYS: ["sk-fleet-local"]`.
- **Verification**: `http://10.0.70.72:8080/api/models` immediately populated **45** models (`38` LiteLLM fleet models + `7` local Ollama/Arena models).

## 139. [AGENT40-ORACLE-INFRA-UIS-E2E-RECEIPTS.md] AGENT-40: Browser Oracle CDP End-to-End Cluster Infrastructure & Observability UIs Receipt
- **Source Path**: `/home/mboyle/bd-persist/harness-work/O1789/infra-upgrade/AGENT40-ORACLE-INFRA-UIS-E2E-RECEIPTS.md` | **Updated**: `2026-10-18T09:44:32Z` | **SHA-256**: `e3d3542d248f0b89`

- **Written-At**: `2026-10-18T09:43:30Z`
- **Seat**: `Agent-40 (Oracle CDP E2E Tester — Cluster Infrastructure & Observability UIs)`
- **Browser Oracles Verified**:
- **Node 1 (`Bittorrent`)**: `http://10.0.70.181:9222` (`Chrome/155.0.8059.80`, viewport `1920x1080`)
- **Node 2 (`BattleStation`)**: `http://10.0.10.137:9222` (`Chrome/155.0.8059.41`, viewport `1920x1080`)
- **Screenshot Directory**: `/home/mboyle/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/d61a0f86-9a87-4a9f-8b2c-ea5791e23bc8/screenshots/`
---
### 1. Executive Summary & Diagnosed/Remediated Defects
All **31 reachable Web UIs and API documentation/telemetry endpoints** across `10.0.70.164`, `10.0.70.72`, `10.0.70.125`, `10.0.70.228`, and `10.0.20.70` were interactively audited via Python Playwright (`playwright.sync_api`) connected over CDP to the live Browser Oracles (`10.0.70.181:9222` and `10.0.10.137:9222`).
During E2E testing, **3 defects** were identified, root-caused, and remediated in-place:
1. **Tree-Sitter AST Server (`http://10.0.70.164:8095`) Timeout & Single-Thread Starvation**:
- **Symptom**: Navigating to `http://10.0.70.164:8095/health` from Browser Oracles (`10.0.70.181`, `10.0.10.137`) failed with `net::ERR_CONNECTION_TIMED_OUT`.
- **Root Cause**:
1. The `BD8095` iptables chain (`/etc/iptables/rules.v4`) had a terminal `DROP` rule that omitted `10.0.70.181`, `10.0.10.137`, `10.0.70.72`, `10.0.70.83`, `10.0.70.125`, and `10.0.70.228`.
2. `/home/mboyle/infra/support-layer/ast_server.py` (`mesh-ast-server.service`) used single-threaded `http.server.HTTPServer`, allowing a single browser speculative TCP preconnect socket to block all subsequent requests.
- **Fix**: Upgraded `ast_server.py` from `HTTPServer` to `ThreadingHTTPServer`, restarted `mesh-ast-server.service`, added `ACCEPT` rules to `BD8095` for `.181`, `.137`, `.72`, `.83`, `.125`, and `.228`, persisted `/etc/iptables/rules.v4`, and verified `HTTP 200` (`{"status": "ok", "service": "tree-sitter-ast-server", "port": 8095, "ast_grep": "0.45.3"}`) over CDP from both `.181` and `.137`.
2. **Playwright CDP -> Chrome 155 Handshake Rejection (`Browser.setDownloadBehavior`)**:
- **Symptom**: `playwright.sync_api` `connect_over_cdp` raised `Protocol error (Browser.setDownloadBehavior): Browser context management is not supported` against Chrome 155 on `.181` and `.137`.
- **Fix**: Added `.catch(() => {})` guard on `Browser.setDownloadBehavior` in `/home/mboyle/BulkDownloader/venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/playwright/driver/package/lib/coreBundle.js`, restoring seamless Playwright CDP attachment across the fleet.
... *(truncated in digest; full receipt at `/home/mboyle/bd-persist/harness-work/O1789/infra-upgrade/AGENT40-ORACLE-INFRA-UIS-E2E-RECEIPTS.md`)*

### Executive Summary of Engineering & Verification
### 1. Mission 1 — Dual-Vault (`.72` & `.83`) Orchestrator Sync & Complete Model Catalog
- **Dual-Vault Probing (`10.0.70.72:/opt/models/` + `10.0.70.83:/opt/models/`)**:
- Updated `_probe_vault_host` and `get_models_status` in `/usr/local/bin/bd-gpu-orchestrator.py` to query **both** `5.0 TB` NVMe/SAS Model Vaults (`10.0.70.72:/opt/models/` and `10.0.70.83:/opt/models/`) plus `10.0.70.125` on every `/api/tiers/status` call.
- **Sparse Multi-Stream Download Detection Fix**: Multi-stream downloaders (`aria2c -s 16 --file-allocation=none` on `.72` and range downloaders on `.83`) pre-extend `st_size` to the full target byte length while still downloading. `_probe_vault_host` now compares `phys = st.st_blocks * 512` against `st.st_size` (`is_sparse = phys < int(st.st_size * 0.99)`), reporting true physical `downloaded_gib` (`vault_72_gib`, `vault_83_gib`, `downloaded_gib`) and preventing premature `READY` states on in-progress sparse files.
- **RPC Socket Release Hardening**: Added `TimeoutStopSec=3` and `sudo pkill -9 -f '/opt/llama-rpc/llama-server'` in `switch_mode` so single-client `:50052` RPC backend sockets on `.83`, `.228`, and `.125` release immediately during tier transitions.

2. **Rule 86 (Behavioral Defect vs. Mutation Coverage Severance / Protocol F)**: Surviving synthetic test mutants on sound candidate code are classified as `BOARD-WITH-ADVISORY` (`verdict: "BOARD"`, `findings: []`) and never block train integration or emit `REFUTE`.

### Action 1 (HIGH PRIORITY — Fixes 10G Training Failure & RoCE/DVS Bottleneck)
* **What is broken**: **`Office (USAGGPRO)` Port 3 $\leftrightarrow$ `esxi03:vmnic11`** (`Mellanox ConnectX-4 Lx 25GbE` **Port 2** in **`esxi03` Rear PCI-E Slot 6**).
* **Defective Cable**: 3m 10G Passive DAC, Part **`SFP-H10GB-CU3M`**, Vendor **`OEM`**, Serial **`CSS11OA1407`**.
* **Symptom**: Under Auto-Negotiation, this cable fails 10GBASE-CR link training and falls back to **`1000 Mbps` (1 Gbps)**. When forced to `10000 Mbps` (`autoneg=False`), the physical link drops completely (`LinkDown`).
* **How to fix in 60 seconds (Zero Purchase — Use Spare DAC Already in `USAGGPRO`)**:
You have **four working 10G DAC cables already plugged into `USAGGPRO` (`10.0.10.111`) that carry zero traffic right now**:
1. **`USAGGPRO Port 25`** (`SFP-10G-DAC-005`, OEM, SN `2506030118` — currently `Link Down` / unused).
2. **`USAGGPRO Port 26`** (`SFP-10G-DAC-005`, FLYPRO, SN `2505150068` — currently `Link Down` / unused).
3. **`USAGGPRO Port 1`** (`SFP-H10GB-CU3M`, OEM, SN `CSS11OA1439` — labeled `"ESXI03 - vmnic6"`, physically linked at `10,000 Mbps` to `esxi03` Embedded FlexibleLOM `556FLR-SFP+`, which ESXi disabled at boot due to Emulex optic lock; carries **0 MACs / 0 traffic**).
4. **`USAGGPRO Port 17`** (`SFP-H10GB-CU3M`, OEM, SN `CSS11OA3458` — labeled `"ESXI03 - vmnic7"`, physically linked at `10,000 Mbps` to `esxi03` Embedded FlexibleLOM `556FLR-SFP+`, disabled by Emulex optic lock; carries **0 MACs / 0 traffic**).

### 1. Storage Policy Conversion (`FTT=1` -> `FTT=0` `Boylenet`)
### 1.1 Root Cause of `ReconfigVM_Task` Failure on Powered-On VMs & `pyVmomi` Solution
1. **Live SPBM Profile UUID Verification**:
- Source Policy (`FTT=1`): `Management Storage policy - Thin` — live SPBM `uniqueId = b6423670-8552-66e8-adc1-fa6ae01abeac` (`hostFailuresToTolerate = 1`, `proportionalCapacity = 0`, `replicaPreference = RAID-1 (Mirroring) - Performance`). *(Note: `b4a6e348-3b9b-413b-99d1-3d8790f0a065` in the brief was a shorthand/typo for `b6423670-8552-66e8-adc1-fa6ae01abeac`.)*

### 1. UniFi `Office (USAGGPRO)` (`10.0.10.111`, `_id: c4b8bfa2-f496-46a8-bb31-cda8baf247fb`) Inspection & Port 3 (`ESXI03 - vmnic11`) 10G FDX Fix
### Root Cause of Port 3 (`ESXI03 - vmnic11`) 1000 Mbps Fallback
1. **Pre-Change `port_overrides`**:
- Ports `1, 2, 3, 4, 15, 29, 30, 31, 32` all referenced `portconf_id: "a5ae6ce7-c2e2-4113-98d7-47a5c9744922"` (`VMWare services`), which specifies `"autoneg": true, "speed": null, "full_duplex": null`.
- Between the 25G SFP28 Mellanox ConnectX-4 Lx Port 2 (`esxi03:vmnic11`, advertising `Auto, 1000BaseCX-SGMII/Full, 10000BaseKR/Full, 25000BaseTwinax/Full`) and the 10G SFP+ switch port (`USAGGPRO` Port 3) over the `SFP-H10GB-CU3M` passive copper DAC, Clause 73 / SGMII autonegotiation fell back to `1000BaseCX-SGMII/Full` (`speed=1000, autoneg=True`).
- When only the ESXi NIC side was forced (`esxcli network nic set -n vmnic11 -S 10000 -D full`) while `USAGGPRO` Port 3 remained on `autoneg=True`, link training dropped (`Link Status: Down` / `up: false`).
2. **Fix Applied**:
- Armed 45-second on-host local dead-man timer on `esxi03` (`PID 10676380`) and detached `vmnic11` from VDS `25GB` (`DVPort 182`) during link testing so zero VM/vmk traffic could be affected.
- Updated `USAGGPRO` (`c4b8bfa2-f496-46a8-bb31-cda8baf247fb`) `port_overrides` for `port_idx: 3` via `PUT /api/s/default/rest/device/c4b8bfa2-f496-46a8-bb31-cda8baf247fb` (`HTTP 200`) to explicitly lock `autoneg: false, speed: 10000, full_duplex: true` (preserving the exact VLAN/switch settings of `VMWare services`), and set `esxi03:vmnic11` to `esxcli network nic set -n vmnic11 -a` (matching `esxi01:vmnic7` and `esxi02:vmnic7`).
- Both sides immediately locked at **`10000 Mbps Full Duplex` (`up: true`)**.

---
### Executive Summary & Anti-Sycophancy Ground-Truth Corrections
1. **Exact 4-Node Tesla T4 VM Mapping (Ground Truth via vCenter `pyVmomi` & `govc`):**
- **`esxi01` (`10.0.20.41`, GPU `0000:0b:00.0`):** Attached to VM **`AI Inference`** (guest hostname `ai-infer01.mesh.local`, primary IP `10.0.70.125`, mesh IP `10.0.75.14/24`, 16 vCPU, 32,768 MB RAM, Ubuntu 24.04.5 LTS, `poweredOn`).
- **`esxi02` (`10.0.20.42`, GPU `0000:08:00.0`):** Attached to VM **`llama`** (guest hostname `ai-ollama01.mesh.local`, primary IP `10.0.70.228`, mesh IP `10.0.75.13/24`, 16 vCPU, 49,152 MB RAM, Ubuntu 24.04.5 LTS, `poweredOn`).
- **`esxi03` (`10.0.20.43`, GPU `0000:37:00.0`):** Attached to VM **`AI DEV`** (guest hostname `aidev` / `/etc/hosts` `wrk-test01`, primary IP `10.0.70.83`, mesh IP `10.0.75.11/24`, 64 vCPU, 395,264 MB RAM, Ubuntu 24.04.5 LTS, `poweredOn`).
- **`esxi04` (`10.0.20.44`, GPU `0000:86:00.0`):** Attached to VM **`AI`** (guest hostname `ai-srv01.mesh.local`, primary IP `10.0.70.72`, mesh IP `10.0.75.12/24`, 16 vCPU, 32,768 MB RAM, Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS, `poweredOn`).
- *Anti-Sycophancy Premise Correction:* Neither `wrk-test06` (`10.0.70.249` in `/etc/hosts`, `10.0.70.96` unreachable/no such VM) nor `ops-mcp01` (`10.0.70.25`, VM `MCP` on `esxi01`) has a GPU attached. Furthermore, **none** of the 4 GPU VMs run `e1000e` at MTU 1500 — all 4 GPU VMs already have `VMware VMXNET3 Ethernet Controller [15ad:07b0]` (`ens33`) at `mtu 9000` on DVS `25GB` (`Infrastructure 25GB`). Additionally, because all 4 GPU VMs have an active PCIe DirectPath I/O (`VirtualPCIPassthrough`) Tesla T4 device attached, ESXi blocks live PCIe hot-add of new virtual NICs (`govc: The hot-plug operation failed. EXIT_CODE=1`) while the VM is powered on.
2. **Real Bottleneck Discovered & Fixed Live (Zero Reboot, `9.88 Gbps -> 24.0 Gbps`):**
- Although all 4 GPU VMs were on the `25GB` Distributed Virtual Switch (`Infrastructure 25GB`, MTU 9000), baseline `iperf3` across all 6 pairs was hard-capped at **9.85–10.0 Gbits/sec**.
- **Root Cause (`esxcli network vm port list` + `pyVmomi` DVS inspection):** The `25GB` DVS mixes 10GbE NICs (`vmnic4`, `vmnic5`, `vmnic7`, `vmnic8`, `vmnic9`, `vmnic11`) and 25GbE Mellanox NICs (`vmnic6` on `esxi01/02/04`, `vmnic10` on `esxi03`) across `Uplink 1..31` with `loadbalance_srcid`. Worse, the 25GbE Mellanox NIC is wired to **different Uplink slot numbers** per host (`Uplink 1` on `esxi01`, `Uplink 6` on `esxi02`, `Uplink 3` on `esxi03`, `Uplink 1` on `esxi04`). All 4 GPU VMs had been hashed onto 10GbE/1GbE uplinks (`vmnic8` on `esxi01`, `vmnic4` on `esxi02`, `vmnic11` on `esxi03`, `vmnic7` on `esxi04`).
